# Preface.

Since this collection, which consists of six volumes under the title:
**Everything shall become New**,
is now intended for those persons who wish to take
deeper looks into the truths of religion and do not
allow themselves to be deterred from reading so-called
mystical writings by unauthorized judgments of condemnation, 
it is superfluous to defend the present edition 
of several essays by the pious Englishwoman, 
**Jane Lead**. <note>Jane Lead (1624–1704) was a prominent English mystic whose visionary writings reached a wide audience in Continental Europe, particularly through German translations that influenced the Pietist movement.</note> One will feel all the less need to do so, 
as the hope is nourished that the so-reproached writings 
of the so-called <term>mystics<note>In this period, "mystics" referred to writers who emphasized the "inner word" and direct personal experience of God over the rigid dogmas and formal rituals of the established state churches.</note></term> will be read more and more and 
will be valued according to their worth by thinking persons 
who are earnest about their eternal <term>bliss<note>The original German "Glückseligkeit" implies a state of profound spiritual happiness and salvation.</note></term>, and who find no satisfaction for the 
intellect and the heart in the <term>conventional way of teaching<note>A critique of "Protestant Scholasticism" or "dead orthodoxy"—the dry, academic approach to theology that many spiritualists felt lacked true life.</note></term>.

We live in a fortunate time when external 
compulsion in religious matters on the part of secular 
and spiritual authorities has been lifted, and every 
citizen is permitted to express his religious opinions freely 
and to present them to his fellow citizens for examination. 
Every person may also choose his reading material 
at his own discretion, and assert his rights as a human 
being in the investigation of religious truths—

<vocab>Preface, Jane Lead, mystical writings, religious freedom, religious truths, bliss</vocab>

<summary>This preface introduces a collection of writings by the English mystic Jane Lead, arguing that seekers of "deeper truths" should ignore traditionalist condemnations and embrace the era's newfound religious freedom.</summary>
<keywords>Jane Lead, Everything shall become New, mysticism, religious freedom, spiritual enlightenment, Pietism, religious tolerance</keywords>

It will come to light more and more that the common religious discourses are no sufficient dam against the encroaching errors, and one will realize that the religion of Jesus Christ must be something more than what is usually said of it; that it must possess an inner, powerfully-acting force that distinguishes it from all other human teachings; and that, in the way it is now commonly taught, it could not possibly have produced that astonishing effect upon the greatest philosophers of the early ages—of which history shows so many examples—upon philosophers and orators who, in sharpness of mind, power of thought, and erudition, are in no way inferior to our modern skilled thinkers, though they expressed themselves differently. <note>The author likely refers to the "Church Fathers" and early Christian converts like Justin Martyr or Saint Augustine, whose intellectual backgrounds were rooted in Greek philosophy before their conversion to Christianity.</note>

For a divine religion must have mysteries that can only be unlocked through the Spirit of God.

<meta>This page continues the author's discussion of how "mystics" view the Holy Scripture. The previous page ended with "They speak of it with," which flows into "the deepest reverence" at the start of this page.</meta>

the deepest reverence, and regard every doctrine as **error** that is not in **harmony** with it. They do not believe, however, that it contains a complete development of all knowledge. Nor is it anywhere stated to be such. It is no ordered **system**, but consists largely of **occasional writings** <note>The original German "Gelegenheit-Schriften" refers to texts written for specific historical occasions or circumstances—such as the letters of the Apostles—rather than a comprehensive, theoretical textbook of philosophy.</note> and could be twenty times larger without being complete for that reason. It is what it is meant to be: a guide to drawing near to God Himself, as the eternal source of all **wisdom**. Moreover, it contains the first elements of all knowledge, the further development and execution of which should depend on the **decree of God** and on the capability and receptivity of humans, to whom the divine Spirit—who inspired the writings of the **Bible**—is ready to grant further insights. For nowhere has God limited His **freedom** in this regard. What He did formerly, He can still do; He will also do it, all the more since He has given the promise that whoever believes in **Jesus Christ** shall receive His Spirit and be guided into all truth by that same Spirit.

But these insights can only be expected from such persons who have entered upon the path that the **Holy Scripture** outlines, and draw all their wisdom and strength from God; from faithful servants of God and sincere **disciples of Jesus Christ**, who want nothing but what God wants, and

In such individuals, one is justified in expecting to find in their writings correct insights into the true meaning of the **Holy Scripture**, especially regarding the highly important **end times** <note>The original German "letzten Zeiten" refers to the "last days" or the apocalyptic era that many contemporary readers believed they were entering during the Napoleonic era.</note>, which we are approaching with rapid strides.

Remarkable events have recently occurred in **Europe**, and we are daily witnesses to occurrences that surpass all previous ones in magnitude and significance, extending even to other parts of the world. We do not yet see what their consequences will be. Only the future will provide us with the correct perspective from which to view them. We were already taught by **Moses** that we only recognize the ways of God through their consequences <note>This refers to the theological interpretation of Exodus 33:23, suggesting that God's plan is often only visible in hindsight ("from the back") rather than while it is unfolding.</note>. We must therefore be very careful not to be hasty in our judgment. Our duty, however, is to take note of everything and compare what is happening with the **revelations of God**, without prejudice, so as not to miss true insight.

Regarding **religion**, one notices that the **apostasy** <term>apostasy (Abfall): a widespread falling away from or abandonment of traditional Christian faith and practice</term> is great and is spreading more and more. It is, however, not yet universal. It is found more among the **scholars** now than in former times. Yet there are still many in silence

The author sees this as a failed "experiment" in living without religion.</note> in **France**—to speak of the abolition of the Christian religion. This abolition, however, will be the great turning point of the **general apostasy** <term>apostasy (Abfall): a total desertion or abandonment of religious faith</term>. The efforts of today's enemies of religion are a more distant preparation for it.

But even when this unfortunate moment arrives, all is not yet lost. For the good Father in heaven, who never forgets humanity and always seeks to save whoever can be saved, will awaken a new, true **Church** from the ruins of the churches that existed before, which shall far surpass all preceding ones in splendor and power.

This highly graced and highly enlightened Church is foretold in the **Revelation of John** <note>Also known as the Apocalypse, the final book of the New Testament.</note>. It is also this Church to which the revelations mostly refer that one finds recorded in the writings of **Jane Leade** <note>Jane Leade (1623–1704) was an English mystic and leader of the Philadelphian Society. Her visions of a future "Virgin Church" were highly influential among German Pietists and theosophers.</note>.

Divine Providence uses it as a means to make people attentive to the **signs of the times**, to inform them of the true character of the final events, and to warn them with fatherly care against the seduction that will then act so universally and powerfully, and is already stirring strongly today. It is necessary to have a firm, inner conviction of one’s faith, wrought by the Spirit of God, and to belong to the **chosen** or the **sealed** <note>Refers to those marked by God for protection during spiritual trials, an image from Revelation 7.</note> in order not to be swept away into **apostasy** <term>Abfall (apostasy): A total abandonment of one's religious faith or spiritual path.</term>. Therefore, one should make use of the many aids to truth which are offered to everyone today...

The Lord has fused the **prophecy** <term>Weissagung: A divine prediction or revelation of future events.</term> of both events together, because the latter was a **foreshadowing** <term>Vorbild: A "type" or "pattern" where a historical event serves as a smaller-scale model for a greater future event.</term> of the destruction of the world.

Blessed is he whom this day does not overtake unprepared!

<meta>This page transitions from the general theological warnings of the preface to a specific biographical account of the English mystic Jane Leade, whose work the German publisher is introducing to his audience.</meta>

## Jane Leade.

In the seventeenth century, there lived in England a very remarkable person, **Jane Leade**, who towards the end of that century also became known abroad, and whose writings appeared in a German translation in Amsterdam in the year 1696. She holds the reputation of having received important revelations from God concerning the **last times** <term>die letzten Zeiten: A theological term referring to the end of the world or the period preceding the Second Coming of Christ.</term>, and her memory is held in great esteem both in England and abroad. She lived at the same time as **Dr. John Pordage** <note>original: "D. Pordädge"</note> and **Thomas Bromley** <note>original: "Thomas Bromlei"</note>, whose writings have also been brought to light in German translations. Along with them, she was a member of a society to which she directed various messages under the title of the **Philadelphian Society** <term>philadelphische Gemeine: A mystical Christian movement founded in London in the late 17th century, named after the "Church of Brotherly Love" in the Book of Revelation.</term>, in which she encouraged the members to walk the path to Paradise—which she described in great detail—with courage and steadfastness. 

She was born in the year 1623, lived for 27 years in a happy marriage with a God-fearing man, and died in 1704 as a widow of 81 years. She was descended from a respected family (Ward, Esq., in the county of Norfolk), and her husband was a very well-to-do merchant. Upon his death, however, she lost almost her entire fortune through the dishonesty of a **factor** <note>A commercial agent or representative who manages trade and goods on behalf of a merchant.</note> to whom her husband had entrusted the greater part of his assets overseas. This resulted in many hardships and frequent trials for her. Yet, as she herself confesses in a letter (*), these trials served to help her gather a lasting treasure in the heavens and, as a **widow of God** <note>A spiritual term for a widow who devotes herself entirely to religious life and relies solely on divine providence.</note>, to hold fast with unbreakable loyalty to her heavenly Bridegroom, from whom alone she sought counsel and help.

---

(*) Letter to the translator of her writings into the German language in Amsterdam, which is appended to her works under the heading: **Life Story of the Author** <note>original: "Lebenslauf der Autorin."</note>.

<summary>This page provides a biographical introduction to Jane Leade (1623–1704), an influential English mystic and leader of the Philadelphian Society, detailing her life, her marriage, and the financial ruin that led to her spiritual devotion.</summary>
<keywords>Jane Leade, Philadelphian Society, John Pordage, Thomas Bromley, Christian mysticism, end times, seventeenth-century England, spiritual trials</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>This page continues the biography of Jane Leade, focusing on her spiritual development following the loss of her wealth described on the previous page. It highlights her mystical practices, her devotion to Scripture, and her perspective on suffering as a divine preparation for spiritual gifts.</meta>

He also provided for her with great faithfulness; He gave her not only what was necessary for her outward maintenance, but also granted her great insights, both regarding His guidance of individual souls and His plan for the guidance of humanity.

Her writings testify to an unfeigned piety <term>Frömmigkeit: A deep, sincere religious devotion that manifests in one's character and lifestyle.</term> and a strong faith.

She practiced daily in self-denial <term>Verläugnung: The spiritual discipline of setting aside one's own ego, comforts, and will to become a vessel for the divine.</term> and in works of love. Truth was dearer to her than anything else, and when she wrote, she placed herself in spirit into the presence of God, so as to say nothing that would not be in accordance with His holy will.

The Holy Scripture was almost her only reading. She viewed it as an inexhaustible source of divine wisdom, which one should never approach without deep reverence and felt gratitude toward its Author.

In particular, however, heart-prayer <term>Herzensgebeth: A form of silent, internal prayer where the believer seeks a direct, emotional, and spiritual union with God, often associated with the "Quietist" movement.</term> was her constant practice, through which she gradually came into such an intimate communion with God that it could almost be said of her what Paul says of Moses (Hebrews 11:27): "She held to the Invisible One as if she saw Him." She attributed these great advantages largely to her external, distressed circumstances, so that she heartily thanked God for that which, for others, would have been a cause for complaint.

> It was given to me to recognize, she says in the aforementioned letter, that the loss of external things and all the crosses and adversities paved a way for me, so that the heavenly powers and gifts could descend into my soul, and caused me to regard as nothing everything that might ever encounter and befall the external person.

The society, of which she became a member as a widow, served as a great strengthening to her in the

<summary>This page details Jane Leade's spiritual life, emphasizing how she viewed her financial loss as a divine path toward "heart-prayer" and mystical insight. It portrays her as a woman of profound discipline who lived in a state of constant internal communion with God.</summary>
<keywords>Jane Leade, heart-prayer, mysticism, self-denial, spiritual growth, adversity, Hebrews 11:27, Philadelphian Society</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page continues the biography of the English mystic Jane Leade, following the description of her inner prayer life. It focuses on her association with Dr. John Pordage and the final days of her life, specifically her spiritual endurance during illness.</meta>

Godliness. She lived for six years with <note>John Pordage (1607–1681) was an English vicar and mystic who, like Leade, was deeply influenced by the writings of Jakob Böhme.</note> Dr. Pordage, who died in the 78th year of his age. She says of him that he left behind the reputation of a divine life and of honest spiritual labor and works of the Lord, adding that she knew no one who possessed such high and excellent knowledge in the deepest mysteries of God.

After the death of Dr. Pordage, the society—which had grown to more than a hundred people—scattered, except for a few who always remained in spiritual connection with one another. To this number belonged, in particular, <note>Thomas Bromley (1629–1691) was a prominent member of the Philadelphian Society and a close spiritual companion to Leade.</note> Th. Bromley, who did not leave her during her final, long-lasting illness, and after her death published an important description of her final hours. This account was published in German in Amsterdam and cannot be read by godly persons without great benefit. (*)

One sees from this account how strongly grace works in the hearts of those who remain still <term>stille halten: Referring to the practice of "Quietism," where the believer remains passive and silent to allow the Holy Spirit to work within them.</term> before it; what virtues it produces; how it arms a person with patience and living hope through the greatest and most persistent physical pains; what humility and love it creates, and how it knows how to keep the spirit free amidst all suffering. As soon as the persistent pains, which the pious widow had to endure on her final sickbed, subsided slightly, she spoke with such cheerfulness as if she were not ill; indeed, one saw how her spirit often asserted its sovereignty <term>Oberherrschaft: The spiritual dominion or mastery of the soul over the physical body and its suffering.</term>, treated the body as a servant, and, as it were, commanded the illness to a standstill.

---

->(*) A detailed and true report of the final hours of Mrs. Jane Leade, composed by one who was a constant eye- and ear-witness <note>original: "Ohren-Zeuge"</note> to them.<-

<summary>This page describes Jane Leade’s six-year residence with the mystic Dr. John Pordage and her later years supported by Thomas Bromley, emphasizing her spiritual triumph over physical suffering during her final illness.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the biography of the English mystic Jane Leade (1623–1704). It moves from the description of her spiritual resilience into the specific physical and mystical trials she faced in her eightieth year and her final decline.</meta>

Her sufferings were far stronger at the end of her life than ever before. In the 80th year of her age, she fell down the stairs while she was home alone and broke her left arm. She was picked up for dead, and for some time, her recovery was doubted. However, she was completely restored and, after three months, was able to perform all her duties just as in her best days of health. Only after a year did a kind of <term>Dörrsucht: A historical term for a wasting disease or consumption, where the body appears to "dry up" or wither.</term> and <term>Gliederschmerzen: Chronic pains in the limbs, likely referring to rheumatism or gout.</term> take hold of her, by which she lay bedridden for many months and finally died.

Yet the inner sufferings and trials she had to pass through, especially during her final illness, seemed even stronger. It was as if she lay in a <term>Läuterungsfeuer: A "purifying fire," used here not as a place after death, but as a metaphorical spiritual state where the soul is cleansed of ego through suffering.</term>, in which her faith was to be tested and purified. The tangible presence of God, which she had otherwise enjoyed, was sometimes completely withdrawn from her, and she felt such a sense of <term>Vernichtigung: A mystical state of "annihilation" or "nothingness," where the believer feels their own total insignificance in the presence of the Divine.</term> that she could not find enough words to express it. She confessed—says the author of the report on her final hours—her nothingness often and at length before God’s countenance, and expressed with emphatic words how dark and unworthy she was to exalt God's light and glory, how ignorant she was to praise His wisdom, and how powerless to glorify His power; she testified that she knew nothing and could do nothing; that God now allowed her to perfectly recognize her own nothingness; and that no one should seek her or look upon her as if she were something. One should respect no creature as if it could provide help or salvation in the day of need, but rather direct all focus alone toward God, as the origin and source of all good, all help, and all truth. What is not drawn from this source leaves us in the end. One should strive, she
said,

<meta>This page continues the account of Jane Leade’s spiritual disposition during her final illness. It moves from her personal sense of "annihilation" or nothingness into her specific advice for others on how to pursue a spiritual life without becoming distracted by human personalities or titles.</meta>

...her <note>Continuing from the previous page's "how powerless to glorify..."</note>, only always toward the heavenly; to always go forward without looking back; that one should never be satisfied with what one knows or does, but should continually seek to grow in knowledge and in doing good. In all prophecies and insights that come from humans, one should not look at the people—who of themselves are nothing and can do nothing—but only at God, who works through them. One should test everything according to the revealed Word <note>Referring to the Bible as the ultimate standard for spiritual truth.</note>, for God cannot contradict Himself; one should seek to explore His intentions and honor Him alone, who shows such great grace to humans through humans.

The only right way to attain perfection, she said, was to seek God for His own sake, purely and with simplicity of heart, without having one’s aim on this world or lingering among men. — She was very distressed if anyone showed her too much reverence and regarded her as if she were an <term>Oracle: A person seen as an infallible source of divine wisdom. Leade redirected such praise away from herself and toward the internal experience of the seeker.</term>; she said one should turn into one's <term>inner ground: In mystical theology, the deepest center of the human soul where God is said to dwell and speak directly to the individual.</term>; there one would find the living oracle and learn to know its voice and distinguish it from all others. She could not stand it at all if people represented her as a prophetess. "Praise God for it," she said, "who chooses people as instruments of His grace according to His free will, without their own contribution. I am a <term>pure nothingness: A state of total humility where the ego is considered void so that God may be everything.</term>, full of darkness, clothed in a body of death; nothing distinguishes me from others except the free grace of God alone, which has been granted to me in abundance."

It is indeed a common mistake that people make, that they look more at the instruments of God than at God Himself, and give honor to men that belongs to God alone, although the great Apostle has already said: Paul is nothing <note>original: "Paulus ist nichts"; a reference to 1 Corinthians 3:7, "So then neither is he that planteth any thing, neither he that watereth; but God that giveth the increase."</note>,

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Jane Leade’s spiritual humility. The previous page ended with Leade describing herself as a "pure instrument." This page opens with a scriptural justification for why God chooses specific individuals for revelation regardless of their personal merit, emphasizing that the "growth" comes from God alone.</meta>

> Apollo is nothing; they may plant and water, but it is God who gives the growth. <note>original: "Apollo ist nichts; sie pflanzen zwar und begießen; aber Gott ist es, der das Wachsthum giebt." A reference to 1 Corinthians 3:6–7.</note>

Another mistake is to view the light and the revelations being shared as a result of the efforts and conduct of human beings. Mankind contributes nothing to this; it is a free gift from God, which He shares with whomsoever He wills. One would be very wrong to believe that Jane Leade was the most pious person in England, Germany, or Europe simply because she received special communications from God. <term>Sanctification: In German, *Heiligung*; the process of being made holy through a life of devotion.</term> can be seen as a consequence of one's conduct, because God has promised this through grace to all who walk according to His commandments, and these promises cannot fail. But special revelations He gives according to His free will, by virtue of His creative power and sovereignty. Thus, by His own free choice, He chose and formed John to be the instrument of His revelations—to be His prophet and, as it were, His own mouth—rather than a Peter, a Paul, or others who were likewise faithfully devoted to Him, without one being able to attribute anything to John for that reason. It is the Lord’s free selection.

If one were to properly consider this rule, many an objection against such persons, and many an offense taken at their external circumstances, would fall away. For here it does not depend at all on the name, gender, status, or person of the one whom God chooses for a special commission. In this, one must look to God alone, who does not let Himself be bound, and be intent only on making use of His grace and purposes. Amos was a shepherd, Ezekiel a priest, David a king, Daniel a statesman, Jonah a common citizen, Isaiah a relative <note>The sentence ends abruptly; historically, Isaiah was often considered to be of royal blood or a relative of King Uzziah.</note>

It uses biblical figures like John the Apostle and various prophets to illustrate that God chooses His "mouthpieces" according to His own will.</summary>

of the royal house; of Elijah <note>original: "Elias"</note> and others we do not know at all who their families were, or what business they pursued before their divine calling. Their external status is completely irrelevant. From God alone come the advantages they enjoyed, and from them merely the faithfulness with which they carried out God’s commissions. Should we look askance because God is exceptionally kind to one or another? Has He not the power to do what He wills? Should we dare to want to argue with Him over it? *I will be gracious to whom I will be gracious.* <note>original: "Wem ich gnädig bin, dem bin ich gnädig." A reference to Exodus 33:19, emphasizing that divine favor is a gift, not a reward.</note> All people are called to <term>salvation: In German, *Seligkeit*; a state of eternal happiness and union with the divine.</term>; but the specific selection as an apostle, prophet, seer, etc., depends solely on the free will of God. Nation, rank, gender, religious party, yes, even the piety of the persons, gives no right to it. God’s reasons for determination lie solely in His wisdom and in His intentions for humanity. We should honor His choice and not seek to judge. He is the Lord, and we are His servants. But we should test from which spirit a teaching or revelation comes, so that we are not led astray by lying spirits.

Jane Leade was, from the time when God chose her as an instrument of His revelations (she herself specifies the year 1670), subjected to great and heavy trials and temptations in spiritual and physical matters, through which she gradually grew into a pure <term>detachment of the mind: In German, *Abgezogenheit des Gemüths*; a mystical state where the soul withdraws from worldly distractions to focus entirely on God.</term> from all sensory and rational things, into a watchful attentiveness to what occurred within her, and into a complete lack of personal will regarding what was revealed to her. <meta>This page continues the biography of the mystic Jane Leade, specifically addressing the spiritual and physical trials she faced after her divine calling in 1670. ...whether the great and wonderful changes would truly occur, by virtue of which a completely perfect state and life could be reached, seen, and recognized here among mortals? Such doubts, which Satan or her own reason stirred within her, may have been caused in part by the cherished opinion that the <term>Philadelphian Society: A mystical Christian movement in late 17th-century England that sought to prepare for a "Church of Brotherly Love" (Philadelphia) before the return of Christ.</term>—in which so many true Christians were found and of which <note>Jane Leade (1624–1704), John Pordage (1607–1681), and Thomas Bromley (1629–1691) were the primary leaders of the Philadelphian movement.</note> Leade, Pordage, and Bromley were members—was destined to represent this extraordinary Church. This was difficult because she perceived that even among the most advanced members of the group, there was much impurity that resisted the unity of the spirit. God strengthened her, however, after each of these <term>temptations: In this context, *Versuchungen* refers to spiritual trials or crises of faith.</term>, and confirmed the prophecies and promises He had given her. Her deathbed confession to Thomas Bromley seems to refer to this premature application of these promises to the times in which she lived and the society to which she belonged. She said: "She had not always fully understood the revelations given to her; she had been too hasty in her hopes, and too quick in making applications or <term>appropriations: The act of claiming specific spiritual promises as being fulfilled in her own person or her specific circle.</term>."

These inner temptations were not the only ones she had to fight against. Her revelations were too extraordinary not to meet great opposition, even from some within her own society, and even more so from others. Added to this was frequent lack of food and many external hardships, which constantly put her faith to new tests. She honors and praises the goodness of her Father in heaven, however, and publicly proclaims that He not only preserved the root of faith within her, but—

<meta>This page continues the biography of the mystic Jane Leade. The previous page discussed her spiritual doubts and the "inner temptations" she faced regarding the Philadelphian Society. ...but also granted ever more strength for growth, with the hope of the completion and fulfillment of everything that was still lacking. These things, she writes, are so great and important that it is well worth waiting for them with earnestness. "If I become a partaker of them," she notes, "they will compensate me for all the damage suffered in sensual, earthly, and transitory things." From year to year, I have found that my life and love for all things transitory has decreased more and more. For since the heavens stand open to me—through which my <term>mind and soul: The German "Gemüthe" refers to the seat of emotions, will, and spirit.</term> and spirit are drawn to the contemplation of the objects to be seen therein and all their independent varieties—I am so captivated by them that I have no need at all to look around outside myself here.

We will now present a few more circumstances from the life of this enlightened woman. Jane Leade was sixteen years old when she was <term>awakened: A term used in Pietism and mysticism to describe a sudden spiritual realization or conversion experience.</term> by God in the following manner. On Christmas Day, she was attending a gathering where they delighted in dancing and making music, when she was suddenly overcome by a deep sadness; it became clear in her mind, as she herself recounts, that this was not the way to heaven. It seemed to her as if she heard these words within her soul:

> “Desist from this; I have another dance that I will lead you through, instead of this vanity.”

She recognized this as the voice of the Spirit of God, immediately left the company, and returned home to the stillness. After some time—having experienced much opposition because of her changed way of life—she confided in a preacher. He advised her to take heart and to believe with certainty that God would help her through every struggle; He had something great and good <note>The sentence ends abruptly here; it likely continued to say "something great and good in store for her."</note>

<meta>This page continues the biographical sketch of Jane Lead, moving from her initial "awakening" at age sixteen to her marriage and the onset of her mature mystical experiences in her mid-forties.</meta>

with her. She therefore remained faithful to the resolution she had made, but had to endure great anguish of soul for three years. The smallest mistakes she had committed, the slightest circumstances where she had failed in word, thought, or deed since childhood, were always before her eyes. Nothing was able to give her comfort or peace until she finally had the opportunity to travel to London to visit her brother; there, she heard a sermon on the **New Covenant** <note>original: "neuen Bund"; a central theological concept in Lead's work regarding a more direct, internal relationship between the soul and God</note>, in which all her objections and all the subjects of her concern were answered and refuted so wonderfully, as if this sermon had been given specifically for her. As much as she would have liked to stay longer in London, where she had made the acquaintance of several godly persons, she nevertheless obeyed the will of her parents, who called her back home. She also did not resist them in their intentions for her to enter into a marriage. However, she declared that she would only give her hand to a God-fearing man. In her 21st year, such a man appeared. It was a relative, William Lead, with whom she lived in a very happy marriage for 27 years and bore four daughters, of whom two died in their childhood, the other two married, but only one outlived her mother.

Even before the death of her husband, in the 45th year of her age, she had an internal appearance <note>or vision</note>, which consisted of special revelations. This gift increased more and more. "For the more I," she says in the mentioned letter, "paid attention to this new grace or communication and ministry of prophecy or revelation, the more it daily manifested itself within me." In this, she fell into embarrassment <note>or perplexity</note>, because she did not know where she should find people who would understand and accept that which...

--- Page 31 ---
<meta>This page continues the biography of Jane Lead, following her return to London and her growing internal spiritual experiences. It transitions from her personal visions to her introduction to the Philadelphian Society and her mentorship under Dr. John Pordage.</meta>

...wished to understand what had been revealed within her. Through searching and inquiry, she finally learned that a society existed in London which, as she expressed it, was engaged in this very same spiritual service and was very earnest in it. This is the society of which we spoke above, and of which **Dr. Pordage**, **Th. Bromley**, and **Mr. Sabberton** were the primary pillars. She calls them men who were highly enlightened in the knowledge of the deep mysteries of God. She particularly describes **Dr. Pordage** <note>original: "D. Pordadge"; John Pordage (1607–1681), an English Anglican priest and mystic</note>, who was both a preacher and a physician, as the instrument through whom she was strengthened, encouraged, and fortified in the communication, the ministry, and the distribution of grace. This pious man was already 72 years old at that time. **Councilor Jung** <note>Johann Heinrich Jung-Stilling (1740–1817), a famous German author and mystic who helped popularize Lead's work in Germany</note> calls him, in his *Pocketbook for Friends of Christianity for the Year 1807*, a thorough <term>theosophist|A person who seeks direct, immediate knowledge of the nature of God and the universe through spiritual ecstasy or individual intuition</term>, whose writings still occasionally have a blessed effect. In the extremely intolerant times in which he lived, he had to endure many persecutions; even then, just as in our own times, people sought to label those who insisted on the internal religion of the heart, on true rebirth, and complete renewal, and who believed in the illumination of the Spirit of God, with all kinds of hateful names in order to keep people from listening to them or reading their writings.

Jane Lead herself is commonly decried as an arch-fanatic <note>original: "Erzschwärmerin"; a derogatory term used by the orthodox church for those claiming direct inspiration from God</note>, although her writings are full of spirit and unction. Councilor Jung, in the aforementioned pocketbook, calls her a highly enlightened woman, and says:

> Whoever reads the writings of this excellent soul without prejudice will soon find that she was entirely illuminated by a ray of heavenly light.

Two years before her death, she delivered for herself a very noteworthy <term>funeral sermon|A sermon preached at a funeral, often reflecting on the deceased's life and faith; here, Lead prepares her own reflections in advance</term> based on Philippians 1:21:
> Christ is my life. <note>original Latin/German: "Christus ist mein Leben."</note>

Her writings deserve great attention for two reasons. First, because they show in great detail what is required to achieve <term>sanctification|The process of being made holy or purified from sin</term>, and what steadfastness, patience, vigilance, and practice in all works of love are necessary to maintain that purity of heart which makes us fit for the resurrection and the Kingdom of God.
> Blessed are the pure in heart, says Jesus; for they shall attain to the vision of God. <note>original: "Selig sind die reinen Herzen... denn sie werden zum Anschauen Gottes gelangen."; Matthew 5:8</note>

She placed a particularly high value on making oneself fit for the resurrection, and taught that one could already receive the <term>resurrection body|A spiritual or glorified body believed by some mystics to be attainable before physical death through spiritual refinement</term> while still in this world, within the coarse earthly body. To achieve this, however, the perfection of the sanctification of the spirit is required, as well as liberation from all impurity, self-centeredness, worldliness, or carnal senses. This is the **rebirth**, which enables the believer to enter the Kingdom of God—a new birth of both soul and body. In this way, Jesus, in the explanation He gave to **Nicodemus** <note>A Pharisee mentioned in the Gospel of John who visited Jesus at night to discuss spiritual matters</note>, connects the spiritual with the physical; He names not only the Spirit from above but also water as necessary for rebirth, since all corporeality arises from the "upper waters" <note>A mystical reference to the "waters above the firmament" in Genesis, often interpreted by theosophists as a divine, heavenly substance or "Sophia"</note>. Therefore, in **baptism**, as the initiation into the covenant of God in Christ, alongside the name of the triune God as the spiritual element, water is also used as an image or <term>vehicle|A medium or carrier through which spiritual forces or substances are communicated</term> of physical rebirth. Without the resurrection body, the rebirth is not complete. We must be reborn in both soul and body to enter the Kingdom of God. Therefore, some had themselves [baptized] over the dead, as if over the grave, in danger of death, when they

...already, as the saying goes, with one foot in the grave, they would still baptize (1 Cor. 15:29), because they viewed baptism as a pledge of the resurrection, in that through baptism one is reborn in both spirit and body. The **resurrection body** <note>A spiritual or glorified body believed by some mystics to be attainable before physical death through spiritual refinement</note> is the great hope of the Christian and their high destiny, which they should strive for with all their might. It completes the redemption, as it is presented in Holy Scripture as the primary part of the **work of redemption** by Jesus Christ, without which His suffering and death would be of no use to us. The main subject of the apostles' preaching was the resurrection; "if in this life only we have hope in Christ" (if we do not believe in the resurrection), "we are of all men most miserable," writes Paul to the Corinthians (1 Cor. 15:19).

Their writings are also remarkable and worth reading because they deal extensively with the end times, which we are approaching with rapid strides. In this regard, several of them are, as it were, a commentary on some important passages of the **Revelation of John**. Their preserved revelations related partly to the <term>millennial kingdom|The thousand-year reign of Christ on Earth prophesied in Revelation 20</term>, and partly to a <term>virgin church|A pure, spiritual community of believers often associated with the 'Bride of Christ' in mystical tradition</term> that would immediately precede it, in which greater deeds and works would occur than have ever happened since ancient times. The members of this church would reach the highest degree of perfection possible for mortal man. Indeed, they would have reached such a degree that, while still in the mortal body, they would be clothed with the resurrection body. To reach this perfection, however, people would have to practice continuously, purify their hearts, and give themselves entirely to God without reservation. However, the death of Dr. Pordage <note>Dr. John Pordage (1607–1681), an Anglican priest and mystic whose visions deeply influenced Lead</note> showed her her error, and the subsequent dispersal of this congregation convinced her that it was not that highly graced church of which the revelations given to her prophesied. She laments this in the introduction to her *Vision of the Tree of Faith and Life*, which she wrote in the year 1686, saying:

> I have waited in secret with earnestness and searched with diligence for where I might find some spirits (spiritual persons) who possessed a true inclination and desire to strive, within the pure temple of their hearts and souls joined in unity, so that the great treasure of holy power (of the day of Pentecost) <note>Refers to the biblical event where the Holy Spirit descended upon the apostles, granting them spiritual gifts and power</note> might once again arise and manifest itself. But until this very day, this has not happened. Each person has been deterred and held back from the other by divisions that occurred—and indeed mostly for the sake of small and vain worldly things—so that up until this hour, no community of saints or Body of the Lord has been able to be brought together. This has pained me greatly and caused me a secret inner suffering and sorrow. In vain I have turned now here, now there, to learn whether the most holy wind might not blow upon one or another, so that at last a beginning might be made for this great and spiritual assembly. However, the serpent <note>In mystical literature, the "serpent" often symbolizes the ego or the divisive influence of the material world</note> immediately destroyed everything that had been prepared.

They had not yet reached the highest degree of faith. They were told to simply continue forward on their path <note>German: *Laufbahn*, literally a "running track" or "career," here referring to the spiritual journey or "race" of faith mentioned in the New Testament</note> and to believe with certainty that the revelations would come to fruition.

These great and powerfully acting forces, she was once told—those which were meant to change the entire course of the visible kingdom in the world—could be entrusted to no one until God became man and man became God, in the same manner as was in our Jesus (p. 47, § 39); <note>This refers to the concept of *theosis* or divinization, a central theme in Lead’s mysticism where the believer undergoes a total transformation to reflect the nature of Christ</note> that is, until the almighty, active power of the Deity had expelled the coarse essence of man—which has its origin in mortal birth—and had filled everything anew through the pouring in of His baptizing Name. For everything must be consumed that is not pure and clean before God (p. 33, § 24). "Therefore," it was added to her, "continue to bring to perfection that which is still lingering in the depths of the soul, from which shall emerge that which will multiply into countless powers."

Meanwhile, Jane Leade's gathered hope nevertheless gradually diminished, and she wrote in the nineties <note>Referring to the 1690s, the decade when Leade published her most significant mystical diaries and established the Philadelphian Society</note> (p. 57, § 47):

> It is not yet clear whether I shall see such things for my own person in my lifetime, or not. Rather, I reckon that I shall fall asleep in faith, like those heroes of faith <note>German: *Glaubenshelden*; a reference to the figures listed in Hebrews 11 who lived by faith without seeing the promises fulfilled</note> who did not reach the fulfillment of the promise, yet saw it from afar and rejoiced in spirit over it. This I also do, and I hope that others in the following times shall recognize this transformation of the bodies (since humans, without suffering physical death, shall receive the resurrection body; namely, when the fruit of faith comes to its full maturity and is eaten in a magical <note>In the context of Leade’s mysticism, "magical" refers to the divine, creative power of the spirit to transform physical matter</note> manner, it transforms even the elementary body and gives it an internally essential ruby-glow <note>German: *Rubinglanz*; a term used in Boehmian theosophy to describe the glorified, crystalline state of the spiritualized body</note>, so that mortality shall be hidden, as if swallowed up, within immortality; this is the true garment that can endure constantly during the thousand years of Christ's Kingdom, and may be called the first resurrection body. p. 55.); I maintain the confidence that I too shall partake in this joy when I have been transitioned into the heavenly world, for I already see my higher angel <note>Leade often spoke of a "heavenly counterpart" or an angelic state of the soul that exists in the divine realm even while the person is on earth</note> clothed over and over in the Kingdom of Mount Zion. — Perhaps it is my lot to come down again with Christ, together with the elders and worthy heroes and heroines of faith, and to reign upon the earth (p. 58, § 47).

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Jane Leade’s realization that she might not live to see the physical manifestation of the "glorious Church." It transitions from her personal reflections to a broader comparison with contemporary mystics and a prophecy regarding the future establishment of the holy community through divine intervention.</meta>

Since she was nevertheless somewhat cast down by the diminished hope of being a witness to the glorious Church in her mortal body, she was told that she and her friends should not, for that reason, abandon the work to which they were called; endure and all of you stand firm in it, and know that it is a special prerogative of God to change the times and hours—He who will not allow Himself to be enclosed within the barriers or limits of time—so that His creatures, who are dependent upon Him, never cease to lie on their knees before Him in prayer and constant vigilance (p. 58).

The same thing that happened to Jane Leade in England is also found in the history of **Antoinette Bourignon** <note>A Flemish mystic (1616–1680) who, like Leade, claimed direct revelation and sought to restore primitive Christianity</note>, who lived at almost the same time. She too wished to found a holy community that would escape the encroaching final judgments and be worthy to receive the Lord at His coming. This latter woman was of the opinion that the island of **Nordstrand** in Denmark <note>An island in the Wadden Sea; at the time, it was a site of religious experimentation and refuge for various persecuted sects</note> was the place of refuge for the holy final Church. However, she died without having formed a congregation, nor having reached this island.

This holy community will most certainly be established. But God Himself will establish it through the **two witnesses** of the **Revelation of John** <note>References the two prophetic figures mentioned in Revelation 11:3</note>, whom Leade calls "two pillars of fire" and, as it were, "brightly burning lights," and through an extraordinary outpouring of the Holy Spirit. (*)

---

(*) With this [outpouring], the Kingdom of God takes its beginning in this world. It is the beginning of the **thousand-year reign** <note>The Millennium; a promised era of peace and holiness on earth</note> following soon thereafter, of which Leade says (in *The Enochian Walks with God* <note>Original title: *Henochische Wandlung im Glauben und Licht* (1694). Leade used the biblical figure of Enoch, who "walked with God," as a model for the mystical state of being translated into the divine realm while still living</note>, § 6, p. 85) that it is the actual kingdom and dominion of the Holy Spirit, which is established on earth once the earth has first been transformed into a paradise (Ibid. § 4, p. 84). Just as in the resurrection of man, mortality is swallowed up by immortality, so too shall the perishability of the earth put on imperishability.

->The<-

# The Tree of Faith
->or<-
## Tree of Life,

->which grows in the Paradise of God, and from which all the wonders of the new creation in the virgin Church of Wisdom’s Firstborn <term>Wisdom: Referring to 'Sophia,' the personified Divine Wisdom central to Behmenist and Philadelphian mysticism</term> must proceed; brought to light by Jane Leade.<-

### From the Author’s Preface.

It has been shown to me that the various adopted forms of worship are nothing but a Babylonian confusion <note>original: "babylonische Verwirrung" - a term used to describe the chaotic and divided state of the various Christian denominations</note>, and that an angel has gone forth from the Chief Shepherd <term>Erzhirte: A title for Christ as the primary leader of the Christian flock</term> to proclaim that all external, human forms of worship—set up by men and arranged through rational inventions (*) —shall vanish like a shadow.

<note>(*) From this it is clear that the Kingdom of God cannot appear as long as human forms of worship—that is, those which now exist—are present. The Kingdom of God cannot merely take place inwardly in the heart; it must manifest itself outwardly.</note>

Existing forms of worship will be destroyed in two ways:

1. Through an extraordinarily graced church, which will perform greater wonders and deeds, and possess higher gifts of grace than any church on earth—not even excepting the apostolic church—has ever had.
2. Through the worship of the Beast <note>A reference to the Antichrist or the worldly powers described in the Book of Revelation</note>, the service of demons, which will flood the entire earth.

<summary>This is the title page of Jane Leade's "The Tree of Faith," featuring a preface that prophesies the end of existing human-made religious institutions. It claims that current forms of worship will be replaced by a miraculous, "graced" church after a period of demonic upheaval.</summary>
<keywords>Tree of Faith, Tree of Life, Jane Leade, Babylonian confusion, Chief Shepherd, Kingdom of God, demonic service, Church of Wisdom, religious reform, prophecy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the numbered list from the previous page's preface, explaining the second reason why existing forms of worship will be destroyed and detailing the nature of the coming "Resurrection-Church."</meta>

...[destroyed], because a day is appearing where nothing but the power and the spirit of the Lamb may endure. For the Church of Christ must appear in the form in which the Lamb himself appeared in his state of resurrection, so that the gathering of His flock for the completion of this Resurrection-Church <term>Resurrection-Church: A mystical concept where the church is not merely an institution but a living manifestation of Christ's risen body</term>, which is born again into faith, will occur.

The new creation, which is to be brought forth, must draw its life’s independence from the faith of the Son of God. His bride and betrothed must be as He was in this world, acting in the quality of the Holy Spirit, and performing those wonders which the final age of the world shall bring to light. For from this newly arisen virgin church <note>virgin church: In Philadelphian and Behmenist thought, this refers to a pure, spiritual community untainted by the "adultery" of worldly denominations and human systems</note>, mighty deeds and works will occur—greater than have ever happened since ancient times. The patriarchs <term>Erzväter: Literally "arch-fathers," referring to the biblical patriarchs like Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob</term>, prophets, and those spirits who possess a high degree of love, power, and purity, will descend again and take such persons to themselves (acting within such persons) who are of this resurrection-life; in this way, according to the lot in which each of the ancient saints stood, they shall carry out glorious deeds.

That church is the immediate preparation for the coming of the Lord, or the 1,000-year kingdom <note>original: "1000jährigen Reiche" — the Millennium, a prophesied thousand-year period of Christ's reign on a purified earth</note> upon the earth and heaven renewed by the punitive judgments of God.

Satan-worship <note>original: "Satansdienst" — here referring back to the "service of demons" or the "worship of the Beast" mentioned on the previous page as the cause of divine judgment</note> is the cause of the terrible judgments which break out over mankind and also strike the earth and the lower heaven, and will transform and make everything new. <margin>Editor's Note</margin>

# The Tree of Faith.

As I recall and consider, at the conclusion of this year 1686, by looking back on what has happened from one new year to the next—both in regard to what I have conquered and attained inwardly in spiritual matters through diligence, and how I have grown therein and progressed from one stage to another; as well as what else has occurred and transpired, through special divine providence <note>divine providence: The belief that God intervenes in the world to guide one’s life toward a specific purpose</note>, in things concerning the outer man; (since for several years now, for my own satisfaction, I have kept a **Memorial** or record of both, and have paid close attention to how the work of God began and progressed within me, to bring to perfection that for which my soul has applied such effort and labor for so many years,) especially, I say, when I consider that which has been made known to me through the **Spirit of Prophecy** since the year 1670, in vision and contemplation; so I have found, from that very year until this approaching new year, that the active wheel has been powerfully turned by the strong hand of the Most High, to bring my soul back into the origin of eternal and pure nature (from which she had fallen in **Adam**). The twelve degrees, limbs, or branches of this **Tree of Faith** <term>Glaubensbaum</term> were then signified and made known to me in their various properties in the following manner:

12. 1) The first degree of this faith is to believe in the **apostasy** <term>Abfall: literally a "falling away"; here referring to the Fall of Man and the subsequent state of separation from God</term> and the ruined essence, which resembles a collapsed pile of rubble, from which the **body of sin** has been drawn together and pulled in (in both soul and body). (*)

2) The second degree is to believe that this apostasy, degeneracy, or **corruption** <term>Verderbung</term> has separated the soul from God, and has alienated it from all (true) knowledge and the possession of these high, precious, and choice things, which could have been the soul's own **rightful property** <term>Eigenthum</term> in God, had she not separated herself from Him and departed.

3) The third degree is to believe that in all the children of men, a **spark or seed of eternity** <term>Saame der Ewigkeit</term> (**) has remained, to move them to incline themselves toward God, to turn inward, and to feel and perceive God within them, as well as to bitterly lament and mourn their fallen state.

(*) One must recognize the corruption within man and in all of outward nature.

10. The way to this, however, is so contrary and opposed to that which is trodden **in practice** <note>original: *in Praxi*</note> by all the children of men in the whole wide world, that to enter upon it or to travel it is a hard and bitter journey—especially since one must, in order to remain constantly upon it, go against the wind and the fast current of nature and completely restrain all its movements, which are so apt to stir, manifest, or **generate** <note>original: *generiren*; likely referring to the natural production of worldly desires or thoughts.</note> themselves. I must persist in resisting these with all steadfastness in faith until victory is wrought through the fully achieved growth of this **most powerful tree**.

11. This [tree] has now been shown to me, as it truly spread out into its boughs and branches in **twelve ascending degrees**, for the revelation of the **ancient apostolic faith**, which in this age is to be revealed anew, for the **shortening of that work** <note>The "shortening of the work" suggests a divine acceleration of the spiritual process, allowing the believer to reach maturity faster than the saints of previous centuries.</note> over which many holy souls have had a difficult struggle and conflict for several thousand years, because they remained stuck and caught in the weak and low degrees of faith. From the consideration of this **apostasy** <term>Abfall: the state of being fallen or separated from the original divine order</term>, I feel a powerful drive and movement within me to press forward in such a manner that I [may bring] all these twelve degrees to

...[that we might] place our whole confidence solely and exclusively upon that which this heavenly **Land of Immanuel** <note>original: *Land Immanuels*; a reference to Isaiah 8:8, used here to describe a spiritual realm of divine presence.</note> provides and brings forth; so that we have no need to look back to where we came from, nor turn back to be tempted once more by the desire for the deadly fruits among the fallen spirits, after we have once been called out from such a state, so that we should live from this precious **Tree of Faith** <note>original: *Baume des Glaubens*</note>;)

so I will reveal by name the various properties of such a tree, as they have been revealed and explained to me, so that we (namely, I and all who stand united with me in this very same spirit) may satisfy our entire inner desire and hunger through them, and may be made perfect in all parts and degrees, when we shall be capable of eating from it in our spirits in a <term>magical manner</term> <note>original: *magische Weise*; in 17th and 18th-century mysticism, "magic" referred to the direct power of the spirit or will to act upon divine substance, distinct from folk sorcery.</note>.

For the **priestly service of the Spirit** <note>original: *priesterliche Dienst des Geistes*</note> is a very delicate and pure matter, which requires exceptionally qualified persons if God is to speak through them. For if anything of **selfhood** <note>original: *Selbstheit*; the ego, individual will, or "self-centeredness" that competes with divine influence</note> should seek to intermingle, it would dampen the burning lamp of the Spirit and cause it to burn weakly and dimly. This is a warning for me and a friend, as well as for those who truly set themselves apart for the purpose of building up a holy **priesthood** and **tabernacle**, in which the **Holy Spirit**, in order to proclaim the **Gospel**, might reveal and manifest Himself with a new source. Through His action, He will cast out the foul waste of all filth born from selfhood, which might otherwise dampen and hinder the coal of the Holy Spirit’s love from burning.

29. After this, I also recalled our **Nazarene vow** <note>original: *nazarenisches Gelübde*; likely referring to the biblical tradition of the Nazirites (Numbers 6), who practiced strict asceticism and separation from the world to be "holy unto the Lord"</note>, wondering whether we had properly kept it or not? I was concerned that, due to any deficiency found in this regard, the **crown of anointing** <note>original: *Krone der Salbung*</note> might be withheld from us. This crown is, and always shall be, the true work and mark of Christ's **anointed** and **most holy priests**, who are called to serve at all times within the veil, in the **Most Holy Place** of the **priestly office**. We must not turn away from this again to defile ourselves with dead and useless things, if we wish to have good progress and prosperity in this high and honorable **confession of faith** <note>original: *Bekenntniß des Glaubens*</note> once pledged, and

<summary>The author warns that "selfhood" or ego dampens the Holy Spirit's light and stresses the necessity of maintaining the "Nazarene vow" of consecration. Only through such purity can one serve as a priest in the "Most Holy Place" and receive the "crown of anointing."</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author’s spiritual reflection on the transition from the restrictive "law" to the freedom of "faith," picking up from the previous page's discussion of Christ as the saving Ark of Faith who must be received into the soul.</meta>

who is the power and the source of faith, was received (or came), we were—as the **Scripture** says in **Galatians 3:23**—held captive under the law. Under what kind of law? Precisely under the law of the **earthly carnal mind** <note>original: *irdischen fleischlichen Sinnes*; referring to a way of thinking driven by physical desires rather than spiritual wisdom</note>, which always stirs up war and strives against the law of faith. Alongside this, we were under the **law of righteousness** <note>original: *Geseze der Gerechtigkeit*</note>, which the Apostle mentions in **Philippians 2:6** <note>The author likely intended to cite Philippians 3:6 or 3:9, which discusses the "righteousness which is of the law."</note>. This law must likewise yield and make room for the law of faith; all righteous works <note>meaning human efforts to be "good" through rules alone</note> must be excluded, because the **righteousness of faith** alone must accomplish the work here. 

If we now turn inward and investigate how things have stood with us, we will find that we have lived in servitude under these two laws, which have ruled over us very strictly. Thus, before faith could be revealed, oh, in what a dark life were we held captive? In that state, neither comfort nor satisfaction was to be found—not even in the most rigorous observation of the law of righteousness, and certainly not in the law of sin and the carnal mind. Therefore, it is high time to look for another law which might redeem us and set us free from these two. 

This is precisely the **revealed law of faith**, which is to become the very nature of a **new birth** <note>original: *neuen Geburt*; a central Pietist and mystical concept of spiritual transformation</note> for us. This is indicated in the aforementioned passage in **Galatians, verse 26**, where we all become **children of God** through faith in **Christ Jesus**, and are thus born from the virginal body of the **Mother of the heavenly Jerusalem** <note>original: *Mutter des obern Jerusalems*; a reference to Galatians 4:26, often interpreted in mystical traditions as the Divine Sophia or the spiritual womb of the soul</note>.

Indeed, through that same natural birth, we were bound under the **laws** of our righteous rational faculty and a modest sensuality. 

For although these things <note>meaning human reason and moral self-control</note> are indeed good in their own way, and we are obligated to show them obedience and submission, this obligation does not extend any further than the moment the royal birth of faith manifests itself.

Which, if you will believe it, said the Word, and if you will earnestly pursue this hope, you will do well; for it has its time to be fulfilled.

41. --- Page 90 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s inner revelation regarding the "mystery of faith" mentioned at the end of the previous page. It explores how spiritual faith operates as an invisible, internal force compared to the visible actions of the physical body.</meta>

The birth of faith, however, was shown to me to be in this manner: that it is full of life, mobility, and activity. Yet everything it effects and does happens in a concealed, secret way. For just as the outer man must exercise his instrumental sensory faculties and powers in a visible way, this spirit of faith, by contrast, exercises all its powers and spiritual faculties most powerfully in a secret, invisible way. It brings about its intended effect even though neither external speech, nor the sound of words, nor visible hands to work, nor feet to walk are necessary for it. Instead, everything lies hidden in the deep center <note>original: *Centro*</note> of a pure mind <term>Gemüth</term> <note>original: *Gemüth*; a central term in German mysticism referring to the seat of the soul, encompassing the heart, mind, and will</note>, which performs and accomplishes great things now on this side, now on that. Yet no ordinary physical being can discover or recognize the basis of this mode of operation. It is known only by the Worker <term>Wirker</term> <note>The "Worker" or "Agent" refers to the spiritual force or the soul acting in union with God</note>, who can testify from experience that it is nothing less than that which lies as deep as God Himself, uniting there with the soul into one body, and going forth with pure effects of power.

42. This revelation and manifestation concerning the mystery of faith was presented to me as the magical key <term>magische Schlüssel</term>, which unlocks the sealed wonders of God’s eternity. Accordingly, my work and task is to use this key in the manner in which I shall be taught and instructed in the art by my Protectress, the highest Wisdom <term>Weisheit</term> <note>referring to *Sophia*, the personified Divine Wisdom often featured in 17th-century mystical writings</note>, who has shown me her various gates and brought me to the same; yes, to me 

<vocab>Faith, Mystery, Revelation, Magical Key, Eternity of God, Wisdom, Mind/Soul, Worker</vocab>

<summary>The author describes faith as a living, invisible force that operates from the soul's center without needing physical tools, acting as a "magical key" to God's eternal wonders. This spiritual power is guided by the figure of Divine Wisdom (Sophia).</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author’s spiritual narrative, shifting from the personal transformation of the body and the "High Angel" to a communal encouragement regarding the timing of divine redemption. she said: although it may appear as if the **staff of hope** <note>original: "Hoffnungsstab." A metaphor for the support found in spiritual expectation.</note> has been broken because of the timing of some expected **redemption** <term>Erlösung</term>, nevertheless, do not let the work to which you are called lie idle for that reason; endure and all stand firm in it, and know that to change the times and hours is God's special prerogative, as one who will not let himself be enclosed in any barriers or limits of time, so that his creatures—who are dependent <note>original Latin-based: "dependirende."</note> upon Him—never cease to lie on their knees before Him in prayer and watchfulness.

Thereupon, after an extensive conversation with the Spirit—which cannot be expressed in words—a wonderfully large **wheel** was presented to me in a **vision** <term>Gesicht</term> <note>meaning a divine revelation or sight.</note>, which seemed to spread out over the entire earth; in color it was **ruby red**, and all around it was full of sharp-sighted eyes. Whereupon it was said to me: that this represented God, the almighty working wheel, which had neither beginning nor end.

After this I saw that a living spirit moved within the wheel, which hung suspended, as it were, between the heavens and the earth. Furthermore, I saw four small wheels descend from this one, which all had the same color and also eyes; whereupon I made this petition: "O Lord! pray tell me, what does this mean?" and it was answered to me: these are the **chariot-powers** <term>Wagenkräfte</term> <note>A reference to the "Merkabah" or divine chariot vision in the Book of Ezekiel, where wheels and eyes symbolize God's omnipresence and movement.</note> that penetrate through all things, the upper and lower **centers** <term>Centra</term> or worlds; and those (among humanity) to whom these wheels are joined, they alone shall become capable of working out the great wonders of the time. Whereupon I saw into which people they descended and rested for a time. But after this, they departed again—

<summary>The author receives a divine exhortation to remain faithful despite delays in spiritual redemption, followed by a mystical vision of a ruby-red wheel covered in eyes, representing God's omnipresent power. and were led upward by the great wheel, which drew them up like a magnet, for then my spirit was instantly with them, and the **angelic center** <term>Centrum</term> or the **angelic world** <note>original: "englische Welt." ## February 10, 1694.

49. This morning the **vision** <term>Gesicht</term> of the **four wheels**, which moved in the **four elements**, appeared again, each of which was led up and into the height by a spirit that was like a bright flash of lightning. When I now contemplated this in my mind <note>original: "Gemüthe," referring to the soul's inner disposition or feeling.</note> and inquired about it, I was answered that these prefigured the active wheels of nature, which the almighty Spirit drove upward, so that they [attain] the one eternal element—

50. While I was now waiting for its opening, it was revealed and explained to me in such a way: that the coarse earthly part in the nature of the four elements should be penetrated by the **Spirit of Christ** (who took on an elementary body), and thus enter into ours; that he should wind them upward by the power of a strong circulating or up-and-down-driving movement, and each part, thus touched and **tinctured** <term>tingirt</term> <note>original: "tingirt." For according to our outer nature, we are all subject to their (the planets’) power and dominion until we have become free by soaring up to this governing upper wheel with its seven eyes. Concerning this, I was told that these were the **seven super-celestial planets**, or constellations and stars, which truly ruled everything in this underworld and in its planets, and held them under their power. This is that which will achieve the **redemption of the body**.

->February 12th.<-

52. ->February 14th.<-

53. While I stood deeply turned inward for the sake of this matter, this word opened within me and spoke: a birth is truly in its breakthrough, which shall bring with it the blessing of a mighty growth for both the present and following generations.

It was said to me: "For what purpose do you suppose this Ark was lowered? Meanwhile, those who despise this call and have no faith to escape must be drowned by another deluge, which, like the waters in **Noah’s** days, will fall upon them from the starry heavens—while the children of the kingdom of **Wisdom**, who have obeyed her voice, will be protected and preserved within, because they have the wings of power (which I had seen given to them beforehand) so that they might fly into this Ark." Within, some who had reached a high degree of faith were received with great celebratory rejoicing and embraces; and these, who entered first, were full of fervent longing to draw others in after them, whom they hoped to see inside.

->February 19.<-

stood there and were received. This was a wondrous place, the likes of which I had never seen; for the paths here were paved over and over as if with translucent gold, and various temples were to be seen, in which these Elders—dressed in long, brightly sparkling priestly robes—showed and paid their respect to the great and mighty King, through whom they performed all their great works and brought into being that which had never existed before. <note>This "King" is likely a reference to the Creator or the glorified Christ, through whom the spiritual "Elders" exercise their divine creative powers.</note> After that, we were led out to behold the pleasant trees and pleasure-groves for our joy and delight: for there the trees seemed to be made of pure, unalloyed gold, and their branches were set, as it were, with diamonds and many kinds of precious stones. We wish to present the contents of the **Ark of Faith**, briefly summarized, for a more convenient overview. From this it will become clear that through this vision, **Jane Leade** <note>A 17th-century English mystic whose visions of "Divine Wisdom" (Sophia) influenced many radical Pietist circles in Germany</note> was given to recognize **Paradise** in heaven as the provisional dwelling place of the saints, but especially the highly favored **Church of the end times**. This Church shall exist for three and a half years <note>original: "viertehalb Jahre"; an archaic German expression for 3.5, referencing the prophetic time period in the Book of Revelation</note> under the two divine prophets and for just as long, after their death and ascension, in the wilderness, in order to be prepared there to receive the Lord when He comes from heaven to establish His Kingdom. ## The marks and characteristics by which the pure, immaculate future Church will certainly be recognized and distinguished from all others are as follows:

The Kingdom of Love is that which shall now manifest itself and will be strong enough to overcome. The thousand-year reign of Christ on earth prophesied in Revelation 20.</term> itself has dawned, the Lord comes visibly from heaven, and the deceased resurrected saints have united with the transformed saints of the **Church of Preparation** <term>German: *Vorbereitungskirche*. The time of grace has passed; all people have made their choice between Him and His adversary. Then the time of justice has arrived, and the Lord now rules with an **iron scepter** <note>A reference to Psalm 2:9 and Revelation 19:15, indicating a rule of absolute authority and judgment over those who resisted the era of grace.</note> over the nations, as over rebellious subjects. This dominion is shared with Him by all His own, all members of His kingdom.

The **first resurrection**, of which Revelation chapter 20 speaks, precedes the Kingdom of Christ. For it will consist entirely of those who have been resurrected.

Before the Church of Preparation, the **spiritual resurrection** <note>This refers to an internal, mystical rebirth of the soul that must happen before any physical resurrection or transformation.</note> takes place, through which its members become capable of attaining bodily resurrection through **transformation** <note>original: *Verwandlung*; refers to the "change" described in 1 Corinthians 15:51-52, where believers are made immortal without experiencing physical death.</note> without dying.

The passage above regarding the Kingdom of Love also refers to this Church of Preparation. <margin>Note by the Editor.</margin>

->The Proclamation of the Three<-

Oh! of the great blessing that without any doubt will rest doubly upon those who set their hands to hewing the first stone on the mountain rock for the foundation of the immaculate church <note>original: *unbefleckten Kirche*. -ssing in these [last] times <note>The syllable "Se-" from the previous section completes the word "Segen" (blessing). J. Leade <note>Jane Lead (1624–1704) was an English mystic and a founding member of the Philadelphian Society. Her visions of "Virgin Wisdom" and the "Everlasting Gospel" were widely read in radical pietist circles in Germany.</note> also indicates that she places the timing of the angel with the **Everlasting Gospel** in the establishment of the highly-graced final church, which will manifest itself in greater power of the Spirit than in the very first Christian church. She says that nothing will be able to resist this Everlasting Gospel. Since the very same thing is said of the **two witnesses** in the Revelation of John, it is probable that the voice of the three angels signifies the doctrine and proclamation of these very prophets, all the more so since shortly before this vision, the prophecy of these two witnesses, the **woman clothed with the sun**, and the first prophecy of the beast precedes it (Chapter 12).

In a theological sense, this refers to God's pre-ordained plan for the salvation of humanity.</note> in Christ Jesus concerning humanity, which is now coming to fulfillment; it concerns the already occurred fall of Babel <note>original: *Babels*. Referring to Babylon, which in this context represents spiritual confusion and the perceived corruption of established church institutions.</note> or ecclesiastical confusion, and finally warns very sharply against sorcery and the worship of the beast, which will flood the entire earth after the death of the two prophets.

Leade <note>Jane Lead (1624–1704) was an influential English mystic whose visions of "Virgin Wisdom" and the restoration of all things gained a significant following among German Pietists.</note> describes the spirit of Elijah, in which the two witnesses appear, in the following way: “he has the power to transform at will and to translate [one] into another being.” (Following the example of Elijah, who raised the son of the poor widow from the dead and gave him a new life. (1 Kings 17:22) The firstfruits <term>Erstlinge: Historically the first part of a harvest dedicated to God; here it refers to the first group of believers to enter the new spiritual kingdom.</term> or forerunners of the new kingdom (the two witnesses) will have this spirit, in order to turn the hearts of the wandering, lost, and prodigal children back to the Father of the eternal spirit, according to Malachi 4:6.)

Therefore, this vision of John also begins with the depiction of the Lamb on Mount Zion, surrounded by the redeemed, with a powerful voice like thunder and the sound of many harps, and with the new song of the four living creatures <note>original: *Lebthiere*. The four symbolic beings described in the Book of Revelation (lion, ox, man, and eagle).</note>, the 24 elders, and the redeemed, who strike up the triumphal song of the Lamb, since the time of His revelation and glorification has now arrived. And so forth.

<summary>This page describes the gathering of "dove-spirits" under a divine banner and discusses Jane Lead's interpretation of the spirit of Elijah and the two witnesses in the end times. It concludes with a vision of the Lamb on Mount Zion and the heavenly celebration of God's final revelation.</summary>
