...of the Savior <note>original: *Heiland*</note> to be summoned, which shall be raised in the Valley of Judgment <note>original: *Gerichts-Thale*; likely referring to the Valley of Jehoshaphat, traditionally identified as the site of the final judgment.</note>.

Wherever the Spirit of faith and love truly finds a constant birth within a person, it will go forth with such a glorious breath <note>original: *Odem*; an archaic term for breath, often implying the divine life-force or "breath of life."</note> or inhalation of power that daily more spirits of the same virginal purity will be added to it. These spirits he is accustomed to preparing for these high degrees of the Glory of Zion; these pure spirits are always looking forward toward the perfect redemption from all the misery to which the earthly image is subject. The divine senses will rise within them and cause them to see and feel under what heavy oppression they lie suppressed, being subject to the Pharaonic spirit <note>original: *Pharaons-Geiste*; a symbolic reference to worldly tyranny and the ego, which holds the soul in bondage as Pharaoh held the Israelites.</note> of this world, who is accustomed to loading them with heavy burdens and imposing a slavish service upon them. Under this they must also endure for a time, so that they may be driven to cry out to the most holy High Priest, so that He might become a Savior of these His "separated ones" <term>Abgesonderten: A term frequently used by radical Pietists to describe those who withdrew from the "corrupt" established churches to form private, holy communities.</term>, for whose sake He now wishes once again, as in ages past, to reveal and display His great wonders.

His mighty hand must be raised to lead them out of Egypt <note>original: *Egypten*</note>, a kingdom that truly prefigures this slavish creation, under which every one of Abraham’s seed <note>original: *Abrahams Saamen*; meaning the spiritual descendants or the faithful children of God.</note> must sigh and be anxious. However, the Most High will awaken a prophet who shall rescue His people from all these Babylonian enchantments <note>original: *babylonischen Bezauberungen*; referring to the spiritual confusion and sensory distractions of the worldly church, which mystics often called "Babylon."</note> through the pure power of spiritual weapons, and liberate them from the tyranny of the Prince of Darkness <note>original: *Fürsten der Finsterniß*</note>, who visibly rules over this world...

For the seed of Abraham must expect to be subjected to distress, just as in former times, until the highest and free sovereignty of the Spirit unites with several holy souls. (*) In this passage, J. <meta>This page continues the discussion from page 127 regarding the leaders who, like Moses and Aaron, are destined to lead the faithful into the promised spiritual rest. It focuses on the "principal powers" and the "Two Witnesses" who prepare the way for the internal and external Kingdom of Christ.</meta>

...had to bring in; likewise, such principal powers must be awakened who stand in great esteem and grace before the Holy Trinity, and who instill terror and fear in the unconverted nations because of the powerfully working force of the Holy Spirit which will rest upon them, and through which they shall pave the way for the redeemed, so that they may return with joy to Mount Zion.

The Kingdom of Christ begins in the Spirit; it precedes the visible revelation of the same, or the 1,000-year Kingdom <term>1,000-year Kingdom: Often called the Millennium; a prophetic period described in the Book of Revelation where Christ reigns in peace.</term>, which Leade (in her *Enochian [Spiritual Cloud and Light]* <note>original: *Henochianiſchen Gl u. L.*; likely referring to a German translation of Jane Leade’s mystical writings, possibly *The Enochian Walks with God*.</note>, p. 85, § 6) actually calls the Kingdom and the sovereignty of the Holy Spirit. This Kingdom begins with the two witnesses <note>The "two witnesses" are prophetic figures from Revelation 11, interpreted by mystics like Leade as spiritual heralds who prepare humanity for Christ’s return.</note> and is prepared through them, so that Christ may appear in His glory.

Their calling is to lead all who follow their call in dove-like simplicity <note>original: *Taubeneinfalt*; a reference to the biblical exhortation in Matthew 10:16 to be "harmless as doves," meaning a state of innocent, guileless faith.</note> into the promised land, and to put all enemies who strive against it into fear and terror. They pave the way for the Kingdom of Heaven in its dawning.

J. Leade also indicates the cause for why people in this world live under such harsh pressure from the dark forces, which she calls "Pharaoh-spirits" <term>Pharaoh-spirits: A mystical term for oppressive spiritual influences that hold the human soul in "Egyptian" bondage or worldly attachment.</term>. They are to be moved by this to cry out to the divine Savior, so that He may redeem them from this bondage, just as the children of Israel cried to the Lord under the heavy yoke of the Egyptians and received a savior in Moses, who led them out of Egypt by the strong hand of the Lord.

This liberation occurs through the awakening of all the powers of God and through the birth of Christ in those who place themselves under His banner.

<summary>This page describes the spiritual preparation for the Millennium, led by "principal powers" and the Two Witnesses who echo the roles of Moses and Aaron. It frames worldly suffering as a tool to drive the soul toward divine redemption from "Pharaoh-spirits."</summary>

<note>original: "Opera," the standard Latin designation for the collected works or publication series of an author.</note>
->BA<unclear>SEL</unclear><-
<note>original: "Basileae," the Latin name for Basel, Switzerland, which was a preeminent center for printing and the book trade in early modern Europe.</note>

<term>binding</term>: the protective cover of a book, consisting of two boards and a spine.
<term>half-bound</term>: a binding technique where the spine and corners are reinforced with leather or sturdy cloth, while the boards are covered in decorative paper.
<term>marbled paper</term>: paper decorated with floating pigments to create stone-like patterns, often used for book covers and endpapers.
<term>spine</term>: the part of the book visible when shelved, typically displaying the title and author.
<term>label</term>: a distinct piece of material (often leather or paper) affixed to the spine to hold printed or stamped text.

<summary>This page records the identifying labels found on the spine of a volume containing the collected works of Gottfried Wilhelm Leibniz, published in Basel. It also provides a glossary of bookbinding terminology relevant to the physical state of the manuscript or book.</summary>
<keywords>Gottfried Wilhelm Leibniz, bookbinding, Basel, Latin translation, academic publishing, bibliography, 18th-century books</keywords>
